011: Repository-check ports for discussion
=========================================

Scope
-----
Port potentially applicable conventions from sibling repositories without
weakening predicates, hiding failures, adding waivers, or repairing production
code to satisfy them. Repository violations remain red for discussion. Changes
to the test launcher only expose required public read-only source inputs and
caller identity; they do not install tools or change model execution.

Source and adaptation records
-----------------------------
The authoritative assertion crosswalks, source revisions, immutable source
links, local mappings, and reasons for domain-specific exclusions are:

- repository_checks/quality/PORTS.md
- repository_checks/science/PORTS.md
- repository_checks/fixtures/containers/source_crosswalk.md

Check modules also document their source and adaptation in place. Source
discovery covered sibling repository_checks, repo-checks, tests/checks,
tests/repository, and tests/repo_checks conventions. The copied project snapshots
under openai-build-week-2026-07 were considered alongside the current standalone
repositories. Application-specific assertions do not create absent providers,
Compose deployments, checkpoint volumes, chemistry parsers, or layered locks.
Their dispositions remain reviewable rather than being counted as passing tests.

Execution boundary
------------------
Use make test/repository. Python, behavioral shell probes, archive fixtures and
Git fixture operations execute inside its offline contained lane. Its grants
remain caller UID/GID, read-only root/public source inputs, no capabilities,
no-new-privileges, two CPUs, 512 MiB memory without swap, 64 PIDs, and a 64 MiB
noexec/nosuid/nodev scratch tmpfs. No engine socket or network is granted.

The host launcher records immediate root-entry types and names with find.
Checkers compare this independent inventory with the mounted public source
tree. A future public source path or cache that is not mounted fails visibly.
Private/control roots .git, .agents, .codex, .aws and acquisition/runtime .work
are classified without disclosing their contents. Only the temporary inventory
file is mounted from .work; the launcher removes it on exit, including failure.

The existing Python base image is reused without pulling or installing. Git,
GNU Make and Ruff 0.15.15 are missing contained prerequisites for some checks.
Scientific Git proofs also require a reviewed read-only Git object database
grant, which the current launcher deliberately does not provide. These failures
must not be interpreted as passing mutation proofs or repository violations.
Tool/image provisioning and Git metadata grants belong to the discussion.

Review and evidence
-------------------
Implementation was delegated by convention family, followed by independent
reviews of a different family. Accepted corrections strengthen the ports:
distinct fake engine ownership identities, actual empty-discovery execution,
strict staging-directory absence, archive-byte parent binding, exact historical
producer inventories, and stronger build-context observations. No production
exception was added to make a check pass.

The first contained run is retained at
.work/qualification/repository-check-port-20261014/initial.log and initial.status.
It ran
224 tests, with 60 failures and 33 errors; it preceded port corrections and is
not the final inventory. Errors include missing contained prerequisites and
port fixture defects, which are distinguished from genuine repository reds.

The unchanged contained unit lane passed all 155 tests after launcher changes.
No checkpoint inference, download, dependency installation, or publication was
performed for this check port.

Reviewed port result before production fixes
-------------------------------------------
The suite contains 261 test methods: the existing 8, plus 129 general, 43
scientific, and 81 container/build/operator checks. The reviewed contained run
finished in 13.422 seconds with 81 failure records and 22 error records; Make
returned 2. Unittest subtests can contribute several failure records from one
method, so these counts must not be subtracted to invent a passing-method count.
Evidence: .work/qualification/repository-check-port-20261014/reviewed.log and
reviewed.status.

All eight effective kernel posture tests, complete public-source visibility,
early shared guard coverage, and the seven-lane native runtime grant matrix
passed. Detector fixtures, corrected Docker continuations, exact ZIP binding,
historical producer inventory mutations and attribution mutations also passed
where they did not require unavailable Git. Independent review of the corrected
code and completed run found no remaining port fixture defect in that pass.

The red results fall into three different categories:

- Failed proof prerequisites: contained Git, Make and Ruff are unavailable;
  committed/historical source proof also lacks Git database grants. Native EOF
  and descendant-retirement scenarios lack a reviewed adapter to a supported
  deadline control, so they fail before dispatch. An outer fixture timeout does
  not establish an operator deadline. No blocked scenario is claimed as passed.
- Observed behavior differences: mutable authored build context and mode/byte
  drift, missing observed import-image admission, inherited engine/proxy/auth
  configuration, malformed container ID authorizing attempted effects, private
  native diagnostics, interrupted status reclassification, split lock/advisory
  publication, raw zero-test discovery, permissive declaration fields and proxy
  port/header/resource/protocol behavior. Some are source or offline injected
  proofs; they do not certify real network routing or live builder behavior.
- Repository-policy gaps: help/default/Make ownership, Python defaults and AST
  posture, caches, Dockerfile/frontend conventions, absent workflow, stricter
  lock header, and missing source/context/attribution declarations. Imported
  checker code is included in applicable scans and can itself be red. Missing
  facts are not fabricated merely to satisfy a schema.

Authorized obvious fixes
------------------------
After reviewing progress, the user authorized delegated obvious fixes while
leaving the remaining choices for discussion. Initial bounded fixes are ordinary
Make help/default behavior and literal ARCHIVE/STAGE admission, closed artifact
and source declaration fields, and proxy destination-port/header handling.
Independent review follows each small coherent change. No blanket style edits,
source-manifest generation, attribution facts, dependency installation, deadline
architecture, or publication is implied by this instruction.

The Make discovery slice adds help to the phony inventory and declares help as
the ordinary default goal. Existing target names and execution recipes stay
unchanged. Its only recipe is shell printf, with no Docker or Python call.
The source checks have a literal overlap conflict: dapp hygiene recognizes only
bare `.DEFAULT_GOAL := help`, while API recipe ownership requires the override
form plus shell/recipe flag overrides. The slice uses the bare explicit default.
It neither duplicates default-goal authority nor changes the imported predicates.
Hostile MAKEFLAGS override policy remains red for discussion.
Host verification ran actual `make` and `make help` with Docker, Python and UV
replaced by rejecting sentinels. Their output was identical; all 19 targets were
advertised and phony, and no sentinel was called. This is command-discovery
proof, not a contained Make prerequisite fix or a model execution claim.

Completed fixes and added regression checks
------------------------------------------
- Make unexports all seven raw selectors: ARCHIVE, STAGE, WLAN_INTERFACE,
  LABEL_ATOL, ATOL, RTOL and RUN_ID. Origin/value aliases preserve command-line
  inputs literally. ARCHIVE/STAGE shell consumers now use only their aliases;
  stage-apply guidance points to the maintained Make command. Unexporting also
  closes GNU Make's implicit export expansion of the already-admitted variables.
  Tolerance numeric validation still belongs to contained baseline_run.py;
  literal preservation does not claim pre-engine numeric validation.
- Release and Uni-Core source loaders reject unknown/missing declaration fields
  before acquisition. Their existing identity and publication semantics remain.
  Duplicate JSON fields, additional metadata value policies and receipt producer
  requirements were not silently claimed as repaired by exact-key validation.
- Proxy destinations require ports 1-65535; explicit HTTP port zero cannot select
  a scheme default. The 64 KiB limit counts header bytes through CRLFCRLF and
  preserves coalesced body bytes. Error diagnostics retain only phase and class.
  Forwarding is marked before its first write attempt, preventing a second HTTP
  status after uncertain writes or CONNECT setup. DNS/deadline/capacity/half-close
  and readiness refinements remain separate discussion items.
- Acquisition startup requires a full lowercase container ID agreeing with its
  regular nonsymlink CID receipt. Receipt comparison is byte-exact: 64 hex bytes,
  optionally one LF. Bash's NUL stripping cannot admit a malformed receipt.
  Interrupted creation can recover identity from its private owned receipt before
  stdout assignment; disagreement between nonempty identities cannot fall back.
  Cleanup refuses an unadmitted ID, retains uncertainty evidence, and retires and
  joins its owned attach client if a started worker's receipt becomes invalid.
- Removed only the observed untracked tools/__pycache__/package_lock.cpython-312.pyc
  and its empty parent. No acquired/model/result evidence was deleted.

Three new repository methods cover literal raw-selector export and actual
ARCHIVE/STAGE delivery. Two further repository methods came from independent
review findings: exact 65-byte NUL-bearing CID rejection and cancellation after
a started worker's receipt is corrupted. Both reach the actual lifecycle phase;
fixture timeout cleanup cannot make them pass. Original assertions and source
predicates remain intact; positive launcher fixtures use the admitted alias
contract rather than restoring ambient input acceptance.

Independent review followed each small implementation bit. The CID byte and
cancellation findings were fixed and exercised before two final independent
reviews found no remaining actionable issue in those changes. This does not
claim the broader remaining repository policies are satisfied.

Final contained evidence after obvious fixes
--------------------------------------------
The maintained unit lane passed all 182 tests in 5.309 seconds, exit 0:
.work/qualification/repository-check-port-20261014/obvious-unit.log and
obvious-unit.status.

The maintained repository lane ran 266 methods in 15.592 seconds, with 75 failure
records and 20 error records; Make returned 2:
.work/qualification/repository-check-port-20261014/obvious-repository.log and
obvious-repository.status. Counts include subtest records, not distinct failed
methods. Its inventory is 8 original, 129 general, 43 scientific and 86
container/build/operator methods. Both new CID regressions, proxy port/header/
failure handling, closed declarations and ordinary Make help/default checks
pass where their execution prerequisites are available.

Focused real-Make evidence used an already-loaded sibling checker image,
sha256:59691117caaac50ea089b0ccc4c27afadf666a6b1b58a78a92540232afcce5b5,
with ten OperatorInputTests passing. The command was Python -B -m unittest -v
repository_checks.test_operator_inputs_port.OperatorInputTests, with entrypoint
python3, workdir /workspace and read-only tools/tests/repository_checks/containers/
Makefile mounts. The container had no network/socket, no image pull, read-only
root, UID/GID 1000:1000, dropped capabilities, no-new-privileges, two CPUs,
512 MiB without swap, 64 PIDs and 64 MiB noexec/nosuid/nodev scratch. The guard,
no-bytecode marker and PYTHONPATH=/workspace were supplied explicitly. Engine
selection used the same four-variable scrub and local Unix endpoint as the
maintained lane. This is a separate focused proof, not a maintained-image change.

Twenty focused launcher/guard/network-gate tests also passed in the maintained
pinned Python image with only tools/tests read-only grants. These two earlier
focused runs were retained in tool output only, not in standalone log files;
their success must not be cited through invented artifact paths. The later full
unit and repository logs above are the retained final evidence. No dependency
installation, acquisition, real checkpoint inference, publication or push was
performed. The code-input hashes are retained in the adjacent source.sha256.

Remaining discussion items
--------------------------
Failed prerequisites remain explicit: Git and its reviewed object-database
grants, Make in the maintained checker image, Ruff 0.15.15, and a reviewed adapter
to supported native deadline control. Blocked Git mutation and native EOF/
descendant scenarios have not become passing proofs.

Remaining behavior differences include mutable inference build inputs and image
admission, engine/proxy/credential environment inheritance, interruption status
handling, lock/advisory publication as one transaction, the empty-discovery
exit-status contract (observed 5 versus imported required 2),
and the unimplemented proxy transport/resource/readiness obligations. Remaining
policy questions include Make override ownership, source/context/attribution
declarations, strict lock header and tool provisioning, Dockerfile frontend/base
notation, missing workflow, Python style/default conventions and Ruff scope.
Some source policies conflict literally or flag newly imported checker code;
those findings are visible for adjudication, with no added waivers.

Concrete log examples and their limits are preserved in
notes/012_repository_check_log_findings_20261014.txt. In particular, empty
discovery already fails; its recorded red is not a successful zero-test lane.

Discussion order
----------------
1. Separate failed prerequisites and port defects from repository-policy reds.
2. Address observed boundary failures: image/engine identity, cleanup and private
   diagnostics, mutable build inputs, proxy behavior, and lock/advisory publication.
3. Decide applicable scientific source/producer/context and lock conventions.
4. Discuss general Make/help/discovery, defaults, AST style, cache and Ruff policy.
   Preserve the original predicates until those decisions are explicit.

The remaining discussion topics are not authorization for broader production
repairs. The obvious fixes above are complete; the working changes remain local
for discussion.

Evidence relocation (2026-10-14)
-------------------------------
The completed check-port captures now reside under
.work/qualification/repository-check-port-20261014/. Captured commands, scripts,
logs and receipts keep their original bytes and embedded historical paths; use
maintained Make commands to rerun checks. The relocation map, before/after hash
inventories and protected-evidence verification are retained in
.work/qualification/tidy-20261014/.

Historical lifecycle observation
-------------------------------
The tidy audit found two running acquisition proxies paired by private CID
receipts with workers that had already exited. Fresh receipt, ownership, mount
and consumer checks admitted retirement of those four containers and their two
private scratch directories. Subsequent independent engine inventory confirmed
their absence. This is an observed historical leftover, not proof of a current
cleanup bug; these receipts do not identify the original executing launcher bytes.

Local evidence is .work/qualification/tidy-20261014/orphan-resources/:
pair-{1,2}/before.txt:1-2, admission.txt:1-5 and after.txt:1-3, with copied CID
receipts and removal-order.txt. summary.txt:1-5 has SHA-256
77abebbfbb9f1004cc50f93bfbedffdc3d4001ffe0642c4ebff4bc4008f67159.

Foundation goal and acceptance (2026-10-14)
-----------------------------------------
Complete the foundation before slice 002. Every applicable repository check and
the unit suite must pass, with independent review and affected qualification.
Treat each check as having a purpose: trace its original obligation and local
owner before changing applicability, assertions or fixtures. Reshape checks only
as necessary for durable local contracts; document the reason and retain the
negative proof. A red requires an explicit disposition, never a silent waiver.

CI is deferred. When introduced, it will cover minimal critical checks. The
imported mandatory-workflow assertion needs an explicit applicability decision
consistent with that deferral; workflow safety obligations remain relevant when
workflows exist. Judge default/style findings by meaning and intent, especially
the distinction between hidden operator choices and justified internal defaults.

Sequence:
1. Provision the dedicated contained Git/Make/pinned-Ruff checker and reviewed
   read-only Git inputs. Acquire dependencies through an explicitly selected
   interface, verify their identities, build offline and record the image ID.
2. Authenticate source/build inventories and actual producer bytes. Preserve
   historical receipts/reference bytes; missing historical proof needs reviewed
   qualification, not invented execution facts.
3. Freeze build inputs and admit observed image identities. Recover paired
   lock/report publication on handled failures without overstating crash or
   concurrent-reader atomicity.
4. Close native execution, environment and proxy findings, then applicable Make,
   discovery, lint and default-policy findings by their actual obligations.
5. Run the complete applicable gate, independently review, repeat affected
   qualification, document exact sources/results and commit coherent slices.

Implementation is delegated in small owned changes, with independent review
after each meaningful bit. All Python, package/archive/model execution and tests
remain contained. Host actions are shell/Git/engine orchestration with explicit
input/output grants; no host dependency installation or implicit acquisition.

Foundation: handled lock/report publication recovery
---------------------------------------------------
The first production repair prepares both candidates and snapshots predecessor
bytes/modes before replacing either fixed output. A handled publication failure
restores consumed members or their original absence. Failed recovery retains
private evidence and annotates the original exception; failure inspecting one
candidate cannot erase that exception or abort recovery of the other member.
This guarantees handled-failure recovery, not atomic concurrent reads or crash
recovery. The source comments cite the exact SECS staging and NMRPeak retained-
tree precedents and explain why their fixed-path/tree contracts differ.

Independent review identified and corrected the candidate-inspection boundary;
focused tests also cover missing expected backups, after-rename interruption,
preparation failure, predecessor modes/absence and cleanup uncertainty. The fresh

Foundation: native execution review and reaping

client only. Metadata admission has its own 30-second deadline and wheelhouse

cancellation guarantee or a total workflow deadline.

pipe after its monitor expired. The ownership anchor now survives TERM until

   tools/docker.sh, before (admission condition; following branch exited 2):
       if ! "${engine[@]}" image inspect "$image" >/dev/null; then

Diagnostic defaults proposal rejected at independent design review
-----------------------------------------------------------------
The proposed comment exception for one AST comparison-symbol conditional would
have kept the original raw matchers and added tokenized, one-node reason tags.
Independent review found that matching the expression's syntax cannot establish
prior type admission or diagnostic-only consumption: the same tagged value can
feed an operational decision. The additional tag machinery therefore does not
prove its promised ownership boundary and is disproportionate to this node.
Disposition: leave the silent-default detector unchanged. Implement and qualify
finite inverse comparison rendering at its existing source owner, preserving
original admission and diagnostic bytes; this source change is pending, not a
passed proof. Exact rejected proposal and its pinned Dapp/API sources:
.work/qualification/python-classification-proposal-20261014/PROPOSAL.md;
manifest d5987fa5b5eed053609cc3747959acb5690d3cdb60b8dcf4198544771ec68a9c.

Untimed native cancellation: TERM opportunity before escalation
---------------------------------------------------------------
Before: actual cancellation immediately KILLed an admitted native group.
After: an actual cancellation already observed at retirement entry offers that
same live held group TERM and one interruptible second before the existing
KILL/join, only when its budget is exactly untimed. Another signal updates the
existing cancellation cause without renewing grace. Late cancellation still
controls the final cause but cannot retroactively promise a TERM opportunity.
TERM delivery or elapsed grace never establishes retirement; KILL/join does.

This deliberate shared policy allows native clients to handle cancellation,
including emitting handler output or Docker forwarding the signal. The concrete
next consumer is acquisition attachment: tools/docker.sh currently TERM-signals
and waits for that client, and its existing corrupt-CID retirement test requires
the actual TERM handler and joined PID. Authority remains the original held
anchor, with no new PID receipt or API. Local client retirement continues to say
nothing about engine-side container removal. tools/container.sh's separate
signal-specific two-second policy remains unchanged.

Numeric calls remain unchanged. Sending TERM into their timed branch would
invoke its expiry handler and could falsely publish deadline evidence. The
one-second precedent is the existing bounded numeric expiry escalation in
tools/inference_native.sh, not a new deadline for scientific work.

Defaults: explicit local contract applicability pilot
------------------------------------------------------
The earlier blanket no-waiver position above was provisional. Purpose-based
adjudication now admits one documented protocol contract through an explicit
local annotation. The original raw candidates, matcher fixtures, discovery and
whole-repository inventory remain intact. This is a reviewed exception, not an
inferred safe context, file exclusion or central approval ledger.

Before:
    path = parsed.path or "/"
    -> raw finding without a consumer-contract decision.
After:
    # silent-defaults: allow-contract -- Origin-form requires / for an empty URI path; proof=tests/test_bound_http_proxy.py::BoundProxyTests.test_empty_absolute_path_becomes_origin_slash
    path = parsed.path or "/"
    -> raw finding retained; operator property accepts this documented contract.

RFC 9112 section3.2.1 requires slash when producing origin-form from an empty
URI path, including a query-only URI. This justifies the existing normalization;
it does not justify a configurable operator fallback or claim full HTTP
conformance (server-wide OPTIONS is separately covered by section3.2.4).
https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9112.html#section-3.2.1
https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9112.html#section-3.2.4
The precise RFC reference is also beside the production annotation and its new
test, which checks exact prepared GET headers for empty and query-only paths.

The property accepts only a standalone actual comment immediately above one
single-line statement with one generic mapping/or/ternary candidate. A reason
and existing local class/test-method reference are required. Static AST lookup
rejects missing, renamed, duplicated or linked proof targets without importing
them. Meaning and actual test execution remain review obligations; symbol
presence is not semantic proof. Environment, CLI, function-default and exception
rules cannot use this annotation. Stale, blank, string, inline, multi-statement
and multi-candidate placements fail. Unannotated neighbouring proof-state
fallbacks remain findings. The existing allow-empty-get convention is the
precedent, not permission to inherit its loose source-substring matching:
repository_checks/quality/silent_defaults/test_python_empty_defaults.py:181-190
and545-575, _has_empty_get_waiver and its annotation fixtures; exact port source:
https://github.com/numpde/vote-mcp/blob/6f595850d199e0d97098c1a7e67ddff774171a25/repo-checks/silent-defaults/test_python_empty_defaults.py

At frozen83e8b48 plus four owning overlays, focused24 pass in0.059s: original
proxy13, one new byte-contract test, seven new applicability tests and three
original raw/inventory tests. Posture20 and conservation pass in0.077s. All
original raw matchers/fixtures, production proxy AST and original unit methods
remain unchanged. Across80 frozen Python files the original and current raw
collectors agree exactly:180 raw findings,179 applicable findings, with only the
documented HTTP node accepted. The actual whole-repository property remains red
as expected. This pilot does not close defaults or overall repo qualification.
Precise source/reference bindings, contained grants/commands, retained setup
failures and complete residual findings:
.work/qualification/default-contract-pilot-20261015/REPORT-v2.txt;
SHA256 11b537f11853f7840d6221ca199dbd3282a38a18f12b53d96eafa34c6bad9c72.

Package input capture owns its utility children

Qualification caught an actual Bash redirection-order error in this bootstrap:

Before:
    if apply_capture=$(umask 077; mktemp ... ) 2>/dev/null; then ...
After:
    if apply_capture=$(umask 077; mktemp ... 2>/dev/null); then ...

The first form redirects too late to cover substitution stderr. Only that

Docker client preparation returns failure before public presentation
------------------------------------------------------------------
Preparation previously wrote utility errors and its summary to public stderr
before the caller could finish cleanup. Merely suppressing those writes would
discard the mkdir cause and let caller cleanup erase its only evidence. The
shared helper now returns controlled docker_client_diagnostic text. Each of its
seven callers saves the failed status before queuing that text and exiting;
existing signal precedence, retirement authority and terminal presentation stay
with the caller. The container launcher retains its separate native owner.

Before:
    if mkdir -m 700 -- "$config"; then :; else
        preparation_status=$?
        printf 'Cannot prepare Docker client configuration ...' >&2
After (the existing private parent owns the attempted receipt):
    umask 077 || exit "$?"
    ulimit -f 4 || exit "$?"
    exec 3> "$receipt" || exit "$?"
    mkdir -m 700 -- "$config" >&3 2>&1

The conditional caller disables errexit inside preparation, so cap and file-open
failures must explicitly stop before mkdir. Failed config setup retains the
existing0700 parent, possible partial config and attempted0600 preparation.log;
the usable-client binding is cleared after constructing its diagnostic. No new
index, directory allocation, retirement fact or cleanup-path mirror is added.
The receipt may be absent or incomplete; a cap failure preserves its observed
wrapper status rather than claiming the utility's intended exit status.

Failed mktemp stdout also cannot grant cleanup authority: its failed branch
saves status and clears docker_client_directory before returning. Without an
admitted directory, allocation failure promises no private cause receipt. A
simultaneous storage failure can prevent the later native reporter from starting;
no builtin fallback or public-delivery guarantee is introduced. Generic filesystem
stalls, allocation/result signal handoff and the container launcher's broader
raw-cleanup/direct-publication limits remain separate work. README adds same-user
discovery of nmrnet-docker-client.* preparation receipts and these limits.

Two cancellation contracts share one held native owner
-----------------------------------------------------
The shared owner previously exposed its existing TERM/one-second policy only.
The container launcher's separate owner forwards the original HUP/INT/TERM and
offers two seconds. Blind substitution would change that contract; duplicating
the held protocol would leave two owners to maintain. Two named entries now
select a required closed policy in the same shell, without result interception:

    inference_native() { native_client term-1s "$@"; }
    container_native() { native_client original-2s "$@"; }

One implementation owns traps, admission, receipts, joining and the existing
result/cancellation/retirement fields. Existing production inference_native
calls stay unchanged. The new entry is preparatory: container.sh still owns its
old protocol until its separately qualified migration.

For original-signal numeric operations, timeout sends private USR2 to its timed
wrapper; that wrapper records the existing expired receipt and attempts TERM
to the held group. Actual group TERM therefore cannot masquerade as deadline
expiry. The existing entry keeps its TERM deadline behavior. Both the timeout
kill-after and the parent's grace use the selected one/two-second policy.
An ordinary client129/130/143 remains distinct from actual cancellation.

The new deadline_grace_started field records entry to a performed wait phase;
it supplies neither a second deadline nor cleanup authority. A known cancellation
skips that expiry wait, while cancellation after entry cannot start a fresh wait.
Repeated signals can change the final cancellation cause without renewing grace.
The phase-entry guard is not an atomic signal handoff, and an expired receipt
does not establish that the client received TERM. Actual delivery and grace must
be observed in qualification. This core slice introduces no container adoption,
new PID authority, ambient policy control or source/API fallback.

The four earned policy methods pass in50.800s, actual tool77897 terminal0.
They observe numeric10 expiry with real USR2/kill-after2 argv, the original
HUP/INT/TERM for numeric and untimed clients, three known/during-expiry/repeated
cancellation phases with one actual sleep2 dispatch, and ordinary129/130/143
without cancellation or expiry. The fixture publishes readiness only after
observing the resistant descendant's actual signal-ignore dispositions. The
known-expiry hook waits for the real TERM marker before HUP; it does not
synthesize expiry.
The same pinned container records GNU timeout9.1, SHA256
5ef0eaaaa4220593add7716aad74da927ca3bb10605e964330de64fecc3ef15e.
Exact /proc executable/argv observations bind the tested binary to that path.

terminal0; the four new green methods are excluded from that run. The original

def count. Adapters preserve every original assertion: three internal launch
strings in two native methods gain the required deadline/grace arguments, and
the grace observer retains its old one-second/three-second defaults. Six strict
anchor-signature strings in five caller test files gain the same fields while
preserving receipt/parent positions, session/group checks and rescue predicates.

Launcher adoption of the shared native owner (2026-10-15, in progress)
-------------------------------------------------------------------

The preceding qualified core commit92a0419 introduced container_native with the
launcher's original-signal/two-second cancellation policy. tools/container.sh
now delegates its offline client, checker inspection, Git/input preparation and
terminal publishers to that owner. This removes its second PID, ready/release,

HTTP content-coding proof before defaults adjudication (2026-10-15)
----------------------------------------------------------------

One new tests/test_artifacts.py method now exercises the current downloader
through actual stdlib HTTPResponse header parsing over finite in-memory wire
bytes. Its seven cases cover absent/explicitidentity at offsets0/17, gzip at both
offsets rejected before reading body bytes while preserving the partial inode,
and same-size damaged resumed identity bytes rejected by MD5 before publication.
Existing Response and all35 prior test bodies remain unchanged; discovery is36.
This is parsed-response/download proof, not a socket or transport claim.

Why this reference/behavior matters: RFC9110 section8.4 requires applied content
codings to be declared; absence therefore means no additional HTTP coding for a
conforming response. Literal Content-Encoding:identity acceptance preserves the
existing compatibility behavior; section8.4 says it SHOULD NOT be sent, so this
is not a required response header. Section12.5.3 defines identity as no encoding.
Section14.1.2 measures ranges in coded bytes: additional coding would change the
archive byte offsets and declared size/digest contract. Header absence does not
prove server truthfulness; final size/digest checks still gate publication.
Precise primary references:
https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-8.4
https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-12.5.3
https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-14.1.2

The contained command passes3 methods in0.006s: the new seven-case method plus
the existing matching-range resume and wrong-checksum/excess-byte methods, each
once. Ruff0.15.15/full-file lint/format and formatter AST parity pass. Exact
original module/Response AST reconstruction,141 other projected files/modes and
all endpoint comparisons pass. An initial Docker-socket sandbox denial occurred
before any container/test; its failed receipt remains separate from the authorized
formatter/focused invocations. Download source, defaults policy and annotations
are unchanged. The earned proof is a preparatory slice, not defaults closure.
Exact frozen inputs, command/exit receipts and conservation scope:
.work/qualification/artifact-content-coding-contract-20261015/REPORT.txt;
SHA256 9e995e0fd3db24da619f89be835b034d9dbacbb04abcfcb6c6df5c508976d713.

Cooperative controller policy in the existing owner (2026-10-15)
--------------------------------------------------------------

A package controller owns nested worker/proxy cleanup. Wrapping it with the
single-client one/two-second escalation could kill it during its own removal
phases. The new controller_native entry therefore requires untimed/stdio and
uses the existing owner/receipts with cooperative completion. It introduces no
second PID authority, success callback or cleanup deadline. The inference_native
and container_native entry contracts remain unchanged.

Before release is attempted, actual cancellation can retire an idle anchor
without waiting for a nonexistent controller. After any attempt, a failed publish
might already have dispatched: only canonical raw completion permits KILL/join.
Missing, malformed or uncommitted completion retains UNKNOWN and forbids the
next owner. Any deadline receipt, including a FIFO or correctly spelled expired,
is invalid for this untimed contract and is rejected without reading it.

Actual original signals are forwarded while the same completion wait remains
in progress; repeated signals do not renew a timeout. An unresponsive controller
can keep this wait live indefinitely. That limit is explicit in the source;
elapsed time cannot establish that its engine cleanup finished. Canonical exit
proves this local group only, never an inner group's retirement or daemon
removal. Package adoption must dispose its unused build configuration beforehand,
retain its stage inputs and skip new parent reporters on nonzero completion.
That separate adoption and the early preflight lifetime gap remain open here.

The five new methods pass14.864s: actual TERM held beyond2s, then INT through the
same finish gate; ordinary143 distinct from actual cancellation; invalid entry
arguments; cancellation before release; release taking effect then returning29;
and NUL/publication/unexpected regular/FIFO-deadline UNKNOWN cases. The new test
module composes the old fixture without rediscovering its55 tests. It retains
unreaped caller identity through process/group assertions and admits only actual
anchor/SID/parent evidence for its failure rescue. Readiness and teardown test
races were corrected in private proposals before runtime.

Eight selected old compatibility methods pass117.252s, scoped20 posture methods
pass0.061s, and formatter/Ruff0.15.15/Bash syntax pass. All runtime methods ran
once without failure. The143-file frozen projection contains the helper and
new test only;141 other base files/modes, old entry bodies and all55 original
native test identities remain exact. This is not a current full55 run, package
composition proof or engine/scientific qualification. A metadata-only absence
assertion was corrected using its existing lone-LF receipt before querying the
remaining owned CIDs; no tests were repeated. All four exact checker CIDs are
absent. Precise inputs, original/scoped methods, actual handles96080/85331,
commands, endpoint windows and retained metadata epoch:
.work/qualification/cooperative-controller-native-20261015/qualification/REPORT.txt;
SHA256 181f9940e4dda41e371da647b11279c21f04b292d41a650b7117926142362f8e.

Package stage hands lifetime to the cooperative controller (2026-10-15)
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Before, packages.sh waited on a foreground docker.sh controller; a parent-only
signal could be deferred while that controller owned worker/proxy cleanup.
The stage now uses the reviewed controller_native policy above:

    controller_native 'package stage controller' untimed stdio "${command[@]}"

It first removes the unused parent build-client configuration, after confirmed
build/admission retirement. Record-only signal traps let that removal finish.
The caller then consumes its path binding even on failure, preventing an EXIT
retry; attempted-path/status text does not claim that any directory survived.
Actual cancellation takes priority and forbids controller dispatch. Any stage
inputs already captured remain with the review stage on every outcome.

The handoff installs a terminal EXIT handler with no removal or helper calls.
Only controller zero restores normal cleanup and permits the Review publisher.
An outer completion proves no inner retirement: even a nonzero controller plus
failed outer evidence removal must not start another parent reporter. The
terminal handler reports returned wrapper diagnostics directly, preserving the
primary status if delivery fails. Direct stderr can block; an unresponsive
controller can keep cooperative waiting live indefinitely. Neither limitation
is hidden by a second timeout, fallback or cleanup owner. README records the
